# Plan: 5. paper

Execution ledger for roadmap entry 5 in `docs/roadmap.md`. One bullet is one step: a part of the entry. A step is built by one dispatch of its executor (a builder agent by default), or run by the orchestrator without an agent (marked). A step is ticked only after its verification commands ran and the whole diff was read; the commands are in `orchestrator-state.md`, and nothing is ticked on inspection. The green checkmark is this file's status vocabulary; everything else in this folder is ASCII. Read `orchestrator-state.md` first after any context compaction.

## Goal

The paper skill, built on academic-paper and run as `/paper <mode>` under academic-paper's mode names on a LaTeX manuscript and its `.bib`: drafting, structure, citations, with the claim check against each source's full text in full, revision and citation-check mode, figures and statistics, the AI disclosure, CRediT, funding and ethics statements, revision patches and the lit-review mode with its literature strategist, which writes a BibTeX entry for each included source from the record it confirmed, as the coverage inventory marks them rebuild, the rows it marked rebuild later included (plan mode and format conversion among them), with the manuscript scripts `anchorize_tex.py` and `apply_tex_patch.py` moved in from `research-hub/tools/manuscript` and `lookup.py` with its test taken from commit 0074728 for checking every reference the skill keeps, every other reference lookup and the `\cite` key check. It applies the `/writing` findings you accept to the manuscript, or has its draft writer judge and apply them when you ask. Its design follows academic-paper's (research-hub's copy, 3.2.0): the agents, phases, cycles and stops are kept as the source has them, and each deviation from them is made only with its reason and your ruling in the plan's Rulings.

## Gate

`anchorize_tex.py`, `apply_tex_patch.py` and `lookup.py` pass their tests in the skill; the `\cite`-against-`.bib` check and the DOI check, both made by `lookup.py`, pass their tests; three side-by-side runs against academic-paper, each compared blind as `docs/dev/blind-comparison.md` says, win or tie: revision on the BSPC round of `research-hub/projects/manuscripts/dongho-ecg-clustering` (the manuscript and `.bib` at research-hub commit f8c5e7176, with `admin/reviews/reviewer-comments.md`), full mode on `research-hub/projects/manuscripts/bttn-incident-af` (`code/`, `RESULTS.md` and `notes/`, with `manuscript/` and `present/` removed), and lit-review on the topic of the Related work section of `research-hub/projects/manuscripts/raw-pls/manuscript/main.tex`, neither side given its `.bib`; the `paper` side of each comparison stops only at the stops its mode lists and asks nothing whose recommendation follows from the run's goal; each entry the lit-review run writes to its `.bib` resolves through `lookup.py resolve`; outline-only, abstract-only, citation-check and disclosure each run once on the revised dongho-ecg-clustering manuscript and you read each output; `/writing` runs on that manuscript, the findings you accept are applied to it by `/paper`, and you read the result; the plan's ledger holds a record for each `rebuild: paper` row that the file of `skills/paper/` the row names holds what the source file did, checked by reading both; the plan's ledger lists every deviation from the design of academic-paper, each with its reason and the ruling that approved it, and nothing else differs, checked by reading both skills.

- The gate: could this pass without the goal being reached? No, every part of the goal has its check: the three scripts and the two `lookup.py` checks by their tests, drafting, structure, citations, figures, statements, revision patches and lit-review by the three blind comparisons and your call on each, the written `.bib` entries by `lookup.py resolve`, the four single modes and the application of the `/writing` findings you accept by your reading of each output, the content of every `rebuild: paper` row by a record read against its source file, and the design by the ledger's list of deviations from academic-paper, each with its ruling, read against both skills. Checked once: yes, each test, comparison and run is made once on the finished work.
- Step 1: could this pass without the goal being reached? No, each script's test runs it on recorded inputs, the cases of the two new `lookup.py` commands are first run on the unchanged tree, where they fail, and the copied scripts' cases follow `research-hub/tools/manuscript/README.md` and the revision patch protocol of coverage row 94.
- Step 2: could this pass without the goal being reached? No, for the part it delivers, the skill's text: each of the 43 `rebuild: paper` rows is read against its source file and the file of `skills/paper/` it names, so a row's content left out fails, each sentence about a script is run against step 1's landed scripts, and the skill is read against `docs/dev/skill-layout.md`. Whether the skill works when run through the Skill tool is step 3's part, since only the pinned skill can be run that way and a builder cannot pin it.
- Step 3: could this pass without the goal being reached? No, for the part it delivers, the kept academic-paper revision output: it is in the ledger with its side command, session id, served model and its questions and answers, read from the side's own run, so an output not produced or not kept fails.
- Step 4: could this pass without the goal being reached? No, for the part it delivers, the rebuilt skill and its figures: a fresh agent compares `SKILL.md` and each role page with academic-paper 3.2.0 mode by mode and lists every deviation with the ruling that approves it, so a deviation no ruling holds is found; the reviewer reads the skill against academic-paper mode by mode and each figure against the comparison page; and a fresh `/paper revision` run on step 3's revision input, given the user's answers there, is read stop by stop against the stops the mode lists, so a stop outside them, or a question whose recommendation follows from the run's goal, fails.
- Step 5: could this pass without the goal being reached? No, your call on each blind comparison, each `paper` side's stops read against the stops its mode lists, the resolve result of each written `.bib` entry, and your reading of the four single-mode outputs and of the applied `/writing` findings are each made on the pinned skill's real output.

- ✅ 5.B The lookups of `lookup.py`: `skills/paper/templates/lookup.py` and `lookup.test.sh` changed so that a lookup is not refused for a rate limit this machine causes: (1) each index host (`api.crossref.org`, `api.openalex.org`, `api.semanticscholar.org`, and the arXiv hosts as now) gets its own turn across all `lookup.py` processes that share the temporary folder, as `arxiv_turn` gives arXiv its turn, with a gap per host that keeps within that host's stated rate, the gap for Crossref set by whether `CROSSREF_POLITE_EMAIL` is set; (2) a reply HTTP 429 with `Retry-After` of 60 seconds or less is retried after that wait, at most 3 times; with `Retry-After` above 60 seconds the index is left out at once with the reason naming the seconds until it resets, and a file in the temporary folder makes every later call of any process skip that index until then; a 429 without `Retry-After` is retried after 2, 4 and 8 seconds; (3) each reply HTTP 200 of an index's metadata interface (a record by DOI or arXiv ID, a title or topic search, references and cited-by) is cached on disk keyed by its URL without any credential, read back for 30 days, never the replies of `check-urls` or `fetch` and never a reply other than 200 (choice C111); the head comment, `docs/dev/scripts.md` where it describes `lookup.py`, `docs/dev/building.md` line 12 where it describes `lookup.test.sh`, and `skills/paper/references/citation-compliance.md` where it has an index printed as left out asked again once and in its row "An outage" state the new behaviour; each of (1) to (3) a case of `lookup.test.sh` run offline (ruling The lookups of lookup.py)

Check, for these six: each changed line read against its ruling and, for the leftovers, against the academic-paper line it cites; `grep -n -i fresh` over `SKILL.md` and `references/*.md` read line by line, each start left being Phase 4a, 4b, 6a or 6b or the agent-gone fallback; the stop's label in each figure read against `SKILL.md`; and the review of ruling 5.Q-no-rerun, read in full (ruling Open item 5.Q-figure) (ruling Open item 5.Q-figure-shape) (ruling Open item 5.Q-citation-restart) (ruling Open item 5.Q-results-check) (ruling Open item 5.Q-agent-starts) (ruling Open item 5.Q-check-loop) (ruling Open item 5.Q-leftovers) (ruling Open item 5.Q-optional-checks) (ruling Open item 5.Q-no-rerun) (ruling Open item 5.R-audit) (ruling Open item 5.R-wordseq)
- 6 the closing: the closing report written (the cost script's output, or that the plan started no agent), the roadmap entry ticked with the gate's output, this folder moved to the archive (orchestrator, no agent) (ruling A)

## Could run in parallel

Independent of each other; the standing rule of one agent at a time still serialises them unless `.agents/plan.yaml`'s `workers_at_once` allows more.

- None: step 2 runs step 1's scripts, and step 3 runs the skill step 2 builds.

## Agents

Each agent a plan skill started for this plan has one bullet, with its agent id, its role and the model the runner served it, except an agent in no role the cost script prices, which has a numbered item under the heading "Agents in no role the cost script prices:"; `/land` writes a step's agents at its booking and when it takes a step back out of main, `/grill` writes its lookup agents, `/spec` writes a brief-check agent it stopped for another model or found dead when it took a run over, the orchestrator writes a brief-check agent it stopped under "The review of a choice", the session that starts a diagnosis agent writes its numbered item right after the start, and `/plan` copies the bullets of a rulings file.

## Blocked, and by what

- None.

### Step 1, Step 0

- Open item B (2026-10-19): step 1, raised by `/spec 5 1` before the brief: rulings D15 and D17 fix what `lookup.py` computes, and no ruling fixes the command form of the new `\cite` key check or of `check-urls` and `quote-check` once they read a manuscript. These are the commands the skill's text and its user run. On the tree, `lookup.py` at 0074728 reads `references.bib` in a report's folder (`git show 0074728:skills/literature/templates/lookup.py`, its head comment, check-urls and quote-check), and the hub's manuscripts name their `.bib` by `\bibliography{refs}` (23 files), `{main}` (16), `{references}` (3) and `{References}` (2), cite with `\cite` (2348), `\citep` (114) and `\citet` (28), and `\input` files with and without `.tex` (`grep -rhoE` over `research-hub/projects/**/*.tex`).
  - (a) The `.bib` named on the command line: `lookup.py cite-keys <main.tex> <file.bib>` prints `missing: <key>` for each cited key the `.bib` lacks and `unused: <key>` for each entry no `\cite` uses, and exits 1 when it prints either; `lookup.py check-urls <main.tex> <file.bib>` prints, for each cited key, the status of its doi, else its eprint, else its url, as now, and for a key with a doi the title ratio of the entry's title to the title the doi resolves to, the first record found in the order Crossref, OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar, with `DOI_MISMATCH` below 0.70; `lookup.py quote-check <integrity record> <file.bib> <folder>` checks the record's passage lines as now. One function reads the cited keys for all three: every command whose name begins with `cite`, its optional `[...]` arguments skipped, in the main file and in every file it `\input`s or `\include`s, a name without an extension read as `.tex`, relative to the main file's folder, with the text after an unescaped `%` left out. Pro: a `.bib` anywhere works, and the three commands share one key reader. Con: one argument more on each call.
  - (b) The `.bib` read from the `.tex`'s `\bibliography{...}` line, with the same commands taking only `<main.tex>`. Pro: one argument fewer. Con: a second thing to parse, a line naming several files, and a manuscript without the line fails.
  - Recommendation: (a), since the file the check reads is then the one named, and `lookup.py` parses only the cited keys. No option is the lazy one; (b) costs more to build.
  - Closed 2026-10-19 under self-rule with (a), C103.

### Step 2, Step 0

- Open item C (2026-10-19): step 2, raised by `/spec 5 2` before the brief: the rulings fix the arguments of each mode (D26), that full mode writes `.tex` and revision edits through anchorize, patch and apply (D25), and where a run keeps its records (D27, ADR 0020), but no ruling fixes what each mode leaves in the manuscript's files, how a mode given only the main `.tex` finds its `.bib`, or how a patch reaches the manuscript. These are what the user gets from each run. On the tree: `apply_tex_patch.py` exits 2 with kind `output` when `--output` is the base and never writes the base (its head comment; case P6 of step 1); `anchorize_tex.py` writes `<tex>.block-manifest.json` beside the file it reads and has no option for another place; on a scratch copy of dongho-ecg-clustering's `manuscript/refs.bib`, `anchorize_tex.py refs.bib --print-blocks` prints `21 blocks`, one per entry (`grep -c '^@'` prints 21); the hub's manuscripts stand in `<project>/manuscript/main.tex` beside `refs.bib` or `main.bib` and name it by one `\bibliography{...}` line (C103's count; `ls` of the three gate projects' `manuscript/` folders).
  - (a) Every change to a manuscript file, the `.tex`, a file it inputs and its `.bib`, is a patch: the file is anchorized, its manifest moved into the run folder, the patch checked with `--check-only`, applied with `--manifest` and `--output <run folder>/<file name>.patched`, and the patched file copied over the file, one patch per file, the run folder keeping each manifest, patch and apply report. Full mode writes `<project>/manuscript/main.tex` and `<project>/manuscript/refs.bib`, refused when `main.tex` exists, the first draft whole and every later change as a patch. A mode given a main `.tex` finds its `.bib` by the one `\bibliography{...}` or `\addbibresource{...}` of the main file or a file it inputs, resolved against the main file's folder with `.bib` added to a name without it; none, or more than one file, is a refusal that names the lines. outline-only writes `outline.md`, the approved outline with its evidence map, into the run folder and changes no manuscript file; abstract-only writes the abstract and keywords into the `.tex` as a patch; citation-check writes `citation-report.md` into the run folder and applies its format corrections as patches; disclosure writes its statements into the `.tex` as a patch at the place the venue's policy gives, and `disclosure.md` in the run folder names the policy and the place; lit-review without a `.tex` writes `review.md` and `refs.bib` into `paper-runs/lit-review-<YYYY-MM-DD>/` of the folder the session runs in, and with a `.tex` writes `review.md` into the run folder beside the manuscript's folder and adds its entries to the `.bib` as a patch. Pro: the manuscript holds each change and every change leaves its manifest, patch and apply report, as row 23 and ADR 0020 ask, and each mode is called with D26's arguments alone. Con: the session copies each patched file over the manuscript file, a write the scripts do not make.
  - (b) The same, except that the patched file stays in the run folder and the manuscript is left as it was, for the user to copy over. Pro: the skill never writes a manuscript file. Con: D40 and ADR 0021 say the accepted findings are applied to the manuscript, and the user then does by hand, after every run, what the run could do.
  - (c) The same as (a), with a `--bib <path>` argument on every mode in place of reading the `\bibliography` line. Pro: no line of the `.tex` is read for it. Con: an argument D26 does not give, which changes a ruling.
  - Recommendation: (a), since it applies what the run changes, keeps the trail ADR 0020 names and needs no argument beyond D26. The lazy option is (b).
  - Closed 2026-10-19 under self-rule with (a), C104.
- Open item D (2026-10-19): step 2, raised by the brief check of `/spec 5 2` (`agents/reviews/2-brief-check.md`, ADRs 4 and Names 1), two choices the user would see. First, D40 and ADR 0021 have the user accept `/writing` findings in one line such as `all but 3, 7`, but the `/writing` report numbers no finding: `skills/writing/SKILL.md:247` to `:255` give the report two parts, groups by rule and findings in document order, and no number, so `3` names nothing. Second, open item C (a) applies each patch with `--output <run folder>/<file name>.patched` and moves the manifest into the run folder under the name `anchorize_tex.py` gives it, while the run folder is to keep each manifest, patch and apply report; full mode patches `main.tex` several times in one run (the draft review rounds, the citation compliance patch, the abstract patch, the `/writing` fixes), and on a scratch copy a second anchorize and move left one `main.tex.block-manifest.json`, one `main.tex.patched` and one apply report, the first trail replaced without warning; the hub's own dongho revision kept one file per round (`admin/reviews/patches/trail/main.rev1.tex` to `main.rev4.tex`).
  - (a) `/writing` numbers its findings 1, 2, 3 in the order its report gives them, across both parts, each finding's number before its first line, a finding that lists several places keeping one number; `paper` reads the user's line against those numbers, `skills/writing/SKILL.md` gaining the rule with its version raised and step 2's paths widened to it. And each patch of a run gets the next number of the run, two digits from 01, and its files are named by it in the run folder's `patches/` folder: `<nn>-<file name>.block-manifest.json`, `<nn>-<file name>.patch.json`, `<nn>-<file name>.patched` and `<nn>-<file name>.patched.apply-report.json`, the file anchorized again before each patch. Pro: the user's line names what the report shows, every later `/writing` report has the numbers for any reader, and the run folder keeps every patch's trail as ADR 0020 says. Con: a change to a second skill in this step.
  - (b) `paper` numbers the findings itself when it shows the `/writing` report, and the patch files are named as in (a). Pro: `/writing` is unchanged. Con: the report the user reads is no longer the one `/writing` printed, so a session that writes no part of a report edits one, and a separate `/writing` run given to `/paper revision` has no numbers.
  - (c) The user names accepted findings by quoting each finding's passage, and the patch files keep C (a)'s names. Pro: no change to either skill. Con: the one-line answer D40 rules becomes many lines, and every patch after a file's first replaces the trail ADR 0020 keeps.
  - Recommendation: (a), since it carries D40's one-line answer as ruled and keeps the trail ADR 0020 and open item C name. The lazy option is (c).
  - Closed 2026-10-19 under self-rule with (a), C105.

### Step 3, Step 0

- Open item H (2026-10-20), kind 3 of `plan-orchestration`'s `references/self-rule.md` (an option that adds a script), raised by the review of step 3 over round 1 (`agents/reviews/3-refuter.md`, "Repair round 1, refuted", Spec 1). Step 3's records were made with commands that no ruling approves: `scratchpad/fix.py`, which takes each `result` message from a session's stream, writes each character outside the rules file's set by its Unicode name in angle brackets, and writes `final-message-turn1.md`, `final-message-turn2.md` and the record's quoted turns; Python heredocs that count the tool calls of each turn by tool name and scan the 1333 tool calls for the strings `research-hub` and `/Users/axelfaes/workspace` (C10); and Python replacements of exact strings in the record, the brief and ADR 0022. The reviewer recomputed every output independently and found each equal. Until the ruling, nothing they produced is used, and step 3 does not land.
  - (a) Approve what they compute, as listed here, for step 3's records. Pro: the records stand as made and checked, and step 3 lands with the fixes at landing. Con: a script ran before its approval.
  - (b) The records made again without those commands: each message copied by hand from the streams and each count made by reading. Pro: no script beyond the approved. Con: 14 messages and 1333 tool calls copied and counted by hand, where a copy or a count can go wrong, for the same content.
  - Recommendation: (a), since each computes one exact answer (a copy, a count, a string match) and the review reproduced every one. The lazy option: none; (b) costs more and gives the same text.
  - Ruled 2026-10-20 by the user: (a).

The brief as drafted had the orchestrator write `accepted-findings.md` from Axel's one-line answer to a separate `/writing` report and give that file to `/paper revision`. `skills/paper/SKILL.md` "### revision" item 1 reads a comments file that is a `/writing` report as items `W<k>`, and only Steps 12's stop "The findings to apply" reads the user's one-line answer, so that route runs neither route of D40 and ADR 0021: `paper` never reads the line, and the comments file is no `/writing` report, against ADR 0021's last sentence.
  - (a) On the copy of research-hub's current revised dongho-ecg-clustering manuscript, after the four single-mode runs, `/writing manuscript/main.tex` runs and its report is saved whole; `/paper revision manuscript/main.tex <that report>` takes it as its comments file, each item `W<k>` changed or named as left unchanged with its reason, and its closing `/writing` review raises "The findings to apply", where Axel answers in one line and the accepted `F<k>` are applied; Axel reads both sets of applied fixes, the diff and the apply reports. Pro: both routes of D40 and ADR 0021 run as the skill builds them, and `paper` reads Axel's line. Con: Axel does not pick among the first report's findings, since every one becomes an item as D40's second route rules, and the run is longer.
  - (b) The brief as drafted: the orchestrator writes the findings Axel accepts to a file of its own and gives it to `/paper revision`. Pro: Axel picks among the first report's findings. Con: neither route of ADR 0021 runs, and the comments file is not the `/writing` report its last sentence names, a contradiction of an ADR in force.
  - (c) Only the closing `/writing` review of the new side's revision comparison counts for D37. Pro: no further run. Con: D37 names the current revised manuscript, which that comparison does not use (it runs on f8c5e7176), so a clause of the step's line goes undone.
  - Recommendation: (a), since it runs D40 and ADR 0021 as ruled and recorded on the manuscript D37 and D39 name. The lazy option is (c).

- Open item F (2026-10-19), kind 3 of `plan-orchestration`'s `references/self-rule.md`, a change to your written rules, raised by the brief check of `/spec 5 3` (F4.8); it does not block step 3. `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md:26` reads "All manuscript and grant-application content (`.tex`, `.bib`, and the `.docx` form it is carried into, budget-table cells and abstracts included) goes through the `academic-paper` skill, and its review through `academic-paper-reviewer`, with their agents dispatched through the Agent tool." Every session loads it, the gate's sides and judges included, and `paper` is built to take academic-paper's place for that work.
  - (a) Leave line 26 as it is through the gate, both sides loading it alike, and decide its wording at the closing, once your calls on the comparisons are in. Pro: both sides run under your real setup, and no rule changes on a result not yet known. Con: the new side reads a rule that names the other skill.
  - (b) Change line 26 now to name `paper` for manuscript content. Pro: the rule names the skill you would use after the plan. Con: the old side then runs under a rule that names the new skill, and the rule changes before the gate's result.
  - (c) Run every side with your global instructions left out. Pro: neither side reads a rule naming a skill. Con: both sides lose every other rule you work under, and the comparison no longer reflects your sessions.
  - Recommendation: (a), since it keeps both sides on equal terms under your real setup and leaves the rule to you once the result is known. The lazy option is (c), which avoids the question by dropping every rule.

### Step 4, Step 0

- Open item I (2026-10-20): step 4, raised by the brief check of `/spec 5 4` (`agents/reviews/4-brief-check.md`, "2. The step line" findings 2, 3, 7, 8 and 10), five choices the user would see.
  - Part 1, the figures of the other skills that launch agents. The ruling "The figures of skills that launch agents" says `docs/figures/` gains a figure of `paper` "and of every other skill that launches agents, once plan 5's fix is done"; step 4's line names `paper`'s figure only, and no step or roadmap entry holds the others (`grep -n -i figure docs/roadmap.md` prints nothing). `README.md:79` names the plan skills, `grill`, `writing`, `code-comments` and `paper` as the skills that launch agents; `docs/figures/pipeline.svg` and `plan-loop.svg` draw the plan skills. `docs/dev/building.md:35` says `gen_figures.py` writes every figure under `docs/figures/`.
    - (a) Step 4 also adds to `docs/figures/gen_figures.py` a figure each of `grill`, `writing` and `code-comments`, drawn as `paper`'s is (its agents in order, its stops with their marks), each shown in `README.md`, and `docs/dev/building.md`'s sentence on the figures says that every skill that launches agents has its figure there and names both scripts. Pro: the ruling is carried out with the fix it names, in the step that already changes `gen_figures.py` and the README's figures. Con: the step grows by three figures, about 30 min of build.
    - (b) A new step between 4 and 5 for the three figures. Pro: step 4 stays as drafted. Con: a second step rewrites the same script and the same README lines right after step 4.
    - (c) Left to a later entry. Pro: no work now. Con: the ruling's sentence goes undone in the plan it names.
    - Recommendation: (a). The lazy option is (c).
  - Part 2, where a failure of the reference checks goes in full, revision and lit-review. Today a `missing:` line of `lookup.py cite-keys` or a failed line of `check-urls` goes to the citation compliance agent (`skills/paper/SKILL.md`, Steps 11), which D43, D48 and D49 remove from those modes.
    - (a) After the last writer pass the session runs both checks; a failure starts the draft writer once with the failing keys and lines, its patch is applied, the checks run again, and a failure left after that is named in the final message with the line as printed. The pass is driven by a script's output, so it is not one of ADR 0022's "at most three writer passes driven by other agents' issues". Pro: the references resolve without a stop and without the user's hands. Con: one more writer start when a check fails.
    - (b) The failures are named in the final message only. Pro: no extra start. Con: the user fixes by hand what the run could fix.
    - (c) A failure is a stop. Con: a stop outside the mode's list, against "The stops of paper".
    - Recommendation: (a). The lazy option is (b).
  - Part 3, the model identifier the AI disclosure names (D47). The configured `reviewer` is the alias `claude:opus` (`.agents/plan.yaml:10`), and `refute`'s Stops define the configured model as the newest model that alias names in the runner's model list.
    - (a) The session gives the draft writer the identifier the alias names in the runner's model list at the run's start, such as `claude-opus-5-5`, the disclosure writes that identifier, and the stop "A model other than the configured one" compares each served model with it. Pro: the disclosure names a model version, and the stop and the disclosure use one value. Con: none found.
    - (b) The alias as written. Con: a disclosure that names `claude:opus`, which is no model's name.
    - Recommendation: (a). No option is the lazy one.
  - Part 4, the statements whose trigger is the ethics check: the responsible-use statement, written when the ethics check rates dual use Moderate or above (`skills/paper/references/statements.md:173`); the reproducibility statement's second trigger, a gap the ethics check marks (`:182`); and a missing approval number, consent or de-identification, which the ethics check lists (`:169`).
    - (a) The responsible-use statement is written when the venue asks for one or the user asks for one at intake, recorded in the Notes; the reproducibility statement when the venue or the user asks for one; a missing approval number, consent or de-identification is named in the draft writer's report and in the final message as a gap for the user to fill; the rating of dual use is left to `researcher`'s ethics check (entry 13). Pro: each trigger is something the rebuilt run has, and no part of the ethics check is done in `paper`. Con: until `researcher` exists a dual-use statement depends on the venue's or the user's ask.
    - (b) The draft writer rates dual use itself from the materials. Pro: the statement is written without an ask. Con: a part of the ethics check comes back into `paper`, against D42 and ADR 0022.
    - Recommendation: (a). No option is the lazy one.
  - Part 5, intake's DOI checking question (`skills/paper/references/intake.md`, "DOI checking"), asked in full, revision, citation-check and lit-review: its Strict answer stops the run at "A reference that cannot be confirmed", which D43, D48 and D49 remove from full, lit-review and revision, and its mark-only answer marks the integrity record, which the rebuilt skill does not write.

Pro: no answer leads to a stop outside a mode's list. Con: full, revision and lit-review no longer offer the choice.
    - (b) Asked in every mode, Strict sending a failed DOI to the draft writer. Con: the answer then changes nothing part 2 does not already do.
    - Recommendation: (a). No option is the lazy one.

### Step 4.A, Step 0

- Open item AY (2026-11-14): step 4.A, a rule clash with ADR 0025, kind 3 of `plan-orchestration`'s `references/self-rule.md` (a contradiction of an ADR in force, and an option that supersedes one), raised by `/spec 5 4.A` at its premise checks.
  - What the tree shows. Step 4.A's line builds `plan` from academic-paper's `agents/socratic_mentor_agent.md`, "the chapter questions with their convergence caps" included. In academic-paper the Socratic mentor holds a dialogue with the user over many rounds: at least 2 rounds per chapter over six chapters, 20 to 30 rounds in a typical run, and an offer to stop after more than 8 rounds on one chapter or more than 30 in all; it tracks the convergence signals of each chapter across those rounds ("Convergence Criteria", "Convergence Mechanism"). ADR 0025 decides "No other agent receives a second message" and names six roles; the mentor and the formatter of `format-convert` (`agents/formatter_agent.md`) are not among them. Every option below adds those two roles, so every option needs a new ADR that supersedes 0025. The formatter's DOCX output runs `pandoc` "when available" in academic-paper; `pandoc` 3.11 is on this machine's PATH (`which pandoc`), and `.agents/plan.yaml` reads `libraries: avoid`.
  - (a) One mentor agent per plan run, started once as a fresh agent and resumed through the runner's message tool with each answer of the user. After each round it writes academic-paper's checkpoint ("Mid-Process Save": chapters done, the chapter in progress, the signals of each chapter, the INSIGHT collection) into the run folder, so a run continued in another session starts a fresh mentor from that checkpoint. The planner is a fresh agent for the outline from the chapter summaries, for each evaluation of the stress test and for the chapter plan. `format-convert` gets a formatter role, a fresh agent started once. A new ADR 0027 supersedes 0025: its decision is 0025's with the mentor (resumed for each answer in plan mode) and the formatter added. Under it, DOCX and Markdown output is made by `pandoc` when it is on the PATH and named in the final message when it is not, as a missing `latexmk` is; a PDF is made only from the `.tex` by `latexmk`, never from a `.docx`. Pros: as academic-paper has it, one advisor keeps the whole dialogue, the signals and what the user said earlier; one start. Cons: the agent's context grows over 20 to 30 rounds; a resumed agent depends on the runner keeping it, which the checkpoint covers.
  - (b) A fresh mentor agent for each round, given the run's dialogue record (the chapter summaries, the INSIGHT collection, the signals of each chapter), with the same formatter, `pandoc` rule and new ADR 0027, but no resumption of the mentor. Pros: ADR 0025's rule that no other agent receives a second message stands; the state of every round is on disk. Cons: 20 to 30 starts, each reading the mentor page and the record again, so each answer of the user waits for a new start; what the user said that the record does not hold is lost between rounds.
  - (c) The session holds the dialogue itself from the mentor's page, as it holds intake; only the planner and the formatter are agents, with the same `pandoc` rule and an ADR 0027 that adds the formatter. Pros: no mentor start, the quickest answer per round. Cons: departs from academic-paper, where the mentor is an agent of its own; the session judges convergence while it also runs the mode.
  - Recommendation: (a), since it keeps academic-paper's mentor as one agent holding the whole dialogue and changes ADR 0025 only by adding the two roles and one named case of resumption. Your ruling on (a) also approves ADR 0027 as worded there and the `pandoc` rule. The lazy option is (c).

### Step 5.A, Step 0

- Open item BG (2026-11-15): step 5.A, a false premise the plan cannot absorb whose options change a rule page (`docs/dev/blind-comparison.md`), kind 3 of `plan-orchestration`'s `references/self-rule.md`, raised by `/spec 5 5.A` at its premise checks.
  - What the tree shows. Three of the revision defects on step 5.A's line are not in the `paper` side's output; the judges saw only a landing or abstract page, while the side read the full text.
    - "ECG-CPC" cited to `Lunelli_2026`: the run read Lunelli's full text (`gate5/revision-1-turn2.jsonl`, line 401), whose Results say "we evaluate state-of-the-art ECG foundation models with publicly available pretrained weights on BenchECG: ST-MEM5, ECGJEPA6, ECG-CPC8,32, and ECGFounder3", and its citation compliance checked the sentence (`citation-report.md:166`, claim 67, VERIFIED). The sentence was already in patch 02 (`grep -c 'ECG-CPC \\cite{Lunelli_2026}'` gives 1 in `02-main.tex.patched`), so `5-revision-fault-trace.md`'s row naming patch 09 and an unsupported citation is wrong.
    - Berger et al. "consistently competitive with two of them, CLOCS and HeartLang": the run read Berger's full text (`revision-1-turn2.jsonl`, lines 3601 and 3606), which says "The randomly initialized encoder is consistently competitive with CLOCS and HeartLang" and evaluates "five pre-trained ECG encoders". The sentence is supported; what stands is that no check ran after patch 15, which wrote it.
    - The HuBERT-ECG filter and scaling "cited to a page the run could not read": the run quoted both from the paper's Methods (`citation-report.md:170`, claim 71, VERIFIED, and its lines 263 and 265).
    - Judge 1 of revision counts the first two among its six failures of `paper` (`5-blind-comparison/revision/judgment-1.md:14-15`), judge 2 the second (`judgment-2.md:20`). Judge 1's verdict rests first on the missing response letter, which the rulings keep out of `paper`; judge 2's on the invented reasons for the model choice and the missing Cliff's delta, which stand.
    - The same page rule leaves many citations of all three comparisons unchecked: the judges report Cloudflare "Just a moment..." pages and empty IEEE pages in full (`full/judgment-2.md:62`, `judgment-3.md:45`, `judgment-4.md:47`) and in lit-review (`lit-review/judgment-1.md:28`, `judgment-2.md:27`). `docs/dev/blind-comparison.md`, step 18, saves "the page of each URL an output cites, as its fetch returns it".
    - The four causes on the line stand: line 276's "ARI at most 0.049" against `results_mitbih.csv:33` (raw-beat HDBSCAN 0.107), no citation check after patches 09 and 15, nothing reading patch 15, and the intake decision at `config.md:39` that left out Cliff's delta and the fourth contrast (`run_analyses.py:47-48`).
  - (a) Step 5.A's line drops the three as defects, with the reason, and keeps its four causes; `5-revision-fault-trace.md` gets its ECG-CPC and Berger rows corrected; `docs/dev/blind-comparison.md` step 18 adds that the orchestrator also saves into the judge's copy each source text a side read in its run (from the side's run folder or transcript), named by the URL or the citation key it stands for, with the files of both skills removed as step 19 says, and that the judge checks a claim against that text when its page shows no more than an abstract or a block; step 5.A's check runs its comparisons that way, and the page's change lands with step 5.A; the revision call stays a loss, since step 5.A runs that comparison again. Pros: each judge checks a claim against what the side read, so a supported citation is not counted as a failure and a wrong one behind a wall is found; the records say what happened. Cons: one more collection step per comparison; a side that saves no texts is checked against its pages only, as now.
  - (b) As (a) for the line and the trace, with `docs/dev/blind-comparison.md` step 31 adding instead that a claim whose saved page shows no more than an abstract or a block is "not verifiable" and never counted as a failure. Pros: no new material in the judge's copy. Cons: those citations stay unchecked in every comparison, so a wrong one behind a wall is never found.
  - (c) Correct the line and the trace only, and leave the page rule as it is. Pros: no rule change. Cons: step 5.A's fresh judges repeat the same false failures and the same unchecked citations.
  - Recommendation: (a), since it gives the judges what the sides read and is the only option under which step 5.A's check can find a wrong citation behind a wall. Your ruling on (a) also approves the sentence of step 18 as worded there. The lazy option is (c).
  - Ruled (2026-11-15): (a), booked in the Rulings as "Open item BG" (the user).
- Open item BH (2026-11-15): step 5.A, a rule clash with ADR 0027, kind 3 of `plan-orchestration`'s `references/self-rule.md` (an ADR clash, and a new command of a script), raised by `/spec 5 5.A` at its premise checks.
  - What the tree shows. ADR 0027 decides that `paper`'s runs "hold no integrity check", that "The integrity check and the ethics check belong to `researcher`", and that the integrity check "covers the references, the claims against each source's full text, the numbers against the saved runs and the figures against their traces". Step 5.A's line asks `paper` to end two causes that are such checks of numbers against saved runs: "the peer reviewer's readings check no number a patch writes against the result file it comes from" (revision, line 276's "ARI at most 0.049" against `results_mitbih.csv:33`, raw-beat HDBSCAN 0.107) and "a description of data handling checked against the code but not against the result files it rests on" (full, 819 of the 17,855 rows of `cohort_icd_h365.csv` with a P-wave duration from 29,500 to 29,899 ms, counted with `awk -F, '$11>=29500 && $11<29900'`). `researcher` is roadmap entry 13 (`docs/roadmap.md:86`), not built.
  - The second choice the brief would take. Ruling "The calls and the repair's scope" approves changing how `lookup.py resolve` confirms an entry. Step 5's run resolved each entry by its title; Kolda_2009, Agueh_2011 and Bonneel_2016 each have a DOI in the `.bib`, and for Kolda_2009 the title search matched another work at ratio 0.94 (Crossref: "Tensor Decompositions: Applications | Algorithmic Aspects of Machine Learning", `gate5/lit-review-resolve-retry2.txt`).
  - (a) A new ADR 0028 supersedes ADR 0027 in one part: in full and revision mode the draft writer records in `intended-claims.md`, for each number it writes, the result file and row it reads it from, and each reading of the peer reviewer checks each number the patches wrote, and each description of how the data were handled, against that file; `researcher` keeps the rest of the integrity check. With it, `lookup.py resolve --entry <refs.bib> <key>`: it reads the entry, resolves it by its DOI, else its arXiv ID, else its title, and prints `CONFIRMED` when the record's title ratio is 0.70 or more and its year equals the entry's, `NOT_CONFIRMED` with the ratio and the year otherwise; lit-review mode confirms each entry it writes this way, and step 5.A's check runs it on the 84 entries. Pros: the run itself finds a number its result file contradicts, as the academic-paper side's own checks do; an entry is confirmed by its identifier and its year, so a title that matches another work is caught. Cons: a part of the integrity check sits in `paper` until `researcher` is built, and the two must not check the same thing twice then; a new command form of `lookup.py`.
  - (b) Step 5.A follows ADR 0027: the draft writer records the file and row of each number it writes, and the peer reviewer's reading checks only that each new number has such a record, not the number; the data-handling description is left to `researcher`; the same `resolve --entry` as (a). Pros: ADR 0027 stands. Cons: a misread number, as at line 276, and the 819 sentinel rows still pass every check of a `paper` run, and step 5.A's comparisons are judged with them.
  - (c) As (a) for the numbers, with no new command: lit-review mode calls `resolve` with the entry's DOI when it has one and its title otherwise, and the title path takes a match only when the year also equals. Pros: no new command form. Cons: the session compares the record with the entry by reading, so a mismatch rests on that reading rather than on a printed result.
  - Recommendation: (a), since it ends both causes inside the run and gives each written entry one printed confirmation. Your ruling on (a) also approves ADR 0028 as worded in (a), with its status `proposed`, and the `resolve --entry` command as worded there. The lazy option is (b).
  - Ruled (2026-11-15): (a), booked in the Rulings as "Open item BH"; ADR 0028 written, ADR 0027 marked superseded in that part (the user).
- Open item BI (2026-11-15): step 5.A, the stop "A step that does not converge" of the `spec` skill: the step stopped twice before its build (open items BG and BH, both ruled (a)), and its brief check (`agents/reviews/5.A-brief-check.md`) finds four more choices that are yours. `/spec` refuses the step until a ruling rewrites, splits or removes it; a ruling on the four parts below rewrites the step's line as each option says. The brief was removed at the stop and a copy kept outside the ledger; the check's other findings are closed in the brief when `/spec 5 5.A` runs again.
  - Part 1, Bonneel_2016 under the `resolve --entry` rule of ruling BH.
    - What the tree shows. Ruling BH takes, for a DOI, the first record found (Crossref first). Crossref's record of 10.1145/2897824.2925918 is titled "Wasserstein barycentric coordinates", without the subtitle the entry has ("...: histogram regression using optimal transport"); `title_ratio` of the two is 0.61, so the rule prints `NOT_CONFIRMED` for Bonneel_2016, while the step's line requires it to print `CONFIRMED`. OpenAlex's record of the same DOI has the full title. Kolda_2009 (ratio 1.00, 2009) and Agueh_2011 (ratio 1.0, 2011) print `CONFIRMED` under the rule.
    - (a) `resolve --entry` takes, in the order Crossref, OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar, arXiv, the first record whose title ratio against the entry is 0.70 or more and whose year equals the entry's, and prints `CONFIRMED against <that index>`; with no such record it prints `NOT_CONFIRMED` with the first record's ratio and year. Pros: an entry whose identifier and year are right is confirmed when any index records its full title, so Bonneel_2016 is confirmed against OpenAlex; a wrong identifier still fails, since every record comes from the entry's own DOI or arXiv ID. Cons: changes the wording of ruling BH; one more comparison per record.
    - (b) Ruling BH as worded; the step's line lists Bonneel_2016 as `NOT_CONFIRMED`, with Crossref's shorter title as the reason. Pros: no change to a ruling. Cons: a correct entry is reported as unconfirmed, and lit-review lists it as a failure in every run that cites it.
    - Recommendation: (a), since it confirms an entry by its own identifier and year whichever index holds its full title. The lazy option is (b).
  - Part 2, the intake answer on numbers from saved results.
    - What the tree shows. The ruling "The causes of the revision faults" names as a cause "the intake reading of 'do not run anything' that forbids a value read from a saved file". The run's intake question 8 was "May the run compute numbers from saved results, and from which files? Recommendation: yes, ... Bootstrap confidence intervals and Cliff's delta can be computed from per-seed ARIs. [no]" (`gate5/revision-1-turn1.jsonl`), and the answer given was "8. No. The code and materials are in code/. DO NOT RUN ANYTHING: no code, no downloads, no experiments; read the code only." (`gate5/revision-1-answer1.txt:9`). The answer refused the computation in its first word. No saved file holds Cliff's delta, the intervals or the Wilcoxon results: `run_analyses.py:47-60` computes them, and no file under `code/results/` holds a Wilcoxon result (`grep -rl -i 'wilcoxon\|p_value\|pvalue' code/results` prints nothing). So the `paper` side followed your answer, and step 5.A's reruns, which give the same answers, cannot report those numbers under any reading of it.
    - (a) Step 5.A's line drops as defects the missing Cliff's delta, the ten-seed intervals and the fourth contrast, and drops the intake reading from the four causes; the revision rerun's judges get a prompt that quotes the answers both sides received word for word, as the ruling "The full call" did for full, so a side that follows "No." is not counted as failing for it. Pros: the skill keeps doing what you answered; the judges see the answer. Cons: the comparison may still prefer a side that computed the numbers against the answer.
    - (b) The reruns give question 8 a new answer that allows computing from `code/results/`. Pros: both sides can report the numbers. Cons: departs from "the same input with the same answers" of the rulings on the reruns.
    - (c) Intake reads an answer that gives "do not run anything" as its reason as allowing the computation, whatever its first word. Pros: no ruling changes. Cons: the skill overrides your explicit "No."
    - Recommendation: (a), since `paper` did what your answer said, and the defect lies in the judges not seeing the answer. The lazy option is (c), which keeps the plan as it is by making the skill read "No." as yes.
  - Part 3, the citation checks after later patches against ADR 0027 and ADR 0023.
    - What the tree shows. The step's line asks for the citation check after each later patch, on the cited sentences that patch adds or changes, and for a check and a reading after the closing findings pass. ADR 0027 decides that the citation compliance agent "runs in full mode before the draft review, in revision mode after the patches", and its Consequences count its starts and the draft writer's passes; ADR 0023 decides that a reply to the closing `/writing` report "starts the draft writer for those findings ... and then the compile again", at the cost of "one patch pass and one compile". The step's text contradicts both: a rule clash, kind 3. The same gap stands in full mode, whose draft-review writer passes and findings pass (full patch 26 in step 5) also write cited sentences, though the step's line names revision only.
    - (a) A new ADR 0029, status proposed, supersedes ADR 0027 and ADR 0023 in that part: in full and revision mode, after each patch that adds or changes a cited sentence once the first citation check has run (revision's second writer pass, the draft-review writer passes, the reference fix, the compile fix and the findings pass), a fresh citation compliance agent checks those sentences only, from the patch file, fetching their sources again into a new temporary folder when the run's folder is gone; after the findings pass a fresh peer reviewer reads the pass's changes, its numbers and its descriptions of the data, an item it records goes to the draft writer once, and the compile follows. Your ruling on (a) approves the ADR as worded here. Pros: no cited sentence of a run goes unchecked in either mode. Cons: departs from academic-paper 3.2.0, which checks citations once in each mode; more agent starts and fetches per run.
    - (b) As (a), in revision mode only, as the step's line words it; full mode keeps the one check of ADR 0027. Pros: the smaller change to academic-paper's design. Cons: full mode's later cited sentences stay unchecked, the same cause.
    - Recommendation: (a), since the cause is the same in both modes. The lazy option is (b).
  - Part 4, the search that stopped while a pass still added sources.
    - What the tree shows. The run did not stop at the 4-pass cap: "After pass 3, three of the five end conditions hold ... The search ended after three passes with three conditions holding" (`5-blind-comparison/lit-review/output-B/run/review.md:51`). academic-paper's rule ("Search must stop when at least 3 ... are met", `literature_strategist_agent.md:453`) ends that search at the same point, so the cause the ruling "A repair step of `paper` before the closing" names is ended only by a rule academic-paper does not have.
    - (a) The search ends when at least 3 conditions hold and condition 2 (the latest pass added fewer than 10 percent of the existing sources) is among them; a pass that adds 10 percent or more always runs another, up to 8 passes, after which a "search limitation" names the conditions that hold; with no condition holding after 4 passes the search ends with a "search limitation", as academic-paper's rule says. The difference is recorded as a deviation from academic-paper. Pros: the search goes on while it still finds much, with its count stated. Cons: departs from academic-paper; more queries per run.
    - (b) `paper` takes academic-paper's rule unchanged (a "search limitation" after 4 passes only when no condition holds), and the step's line drops this cause, since academic-paper's own rule ends the run's search at the same pass. Pros: no deviation. Cons: the search still ends while a pass adds 25 sources to 59.
    - Recommendation: (a), since it ends the cause your ruling names. The lazy option is (b).
  - Ruled (2026-11-15): 1 (a), 2 (a), 3 (a), 4 (a), booked in the Rulings as "Open item BI"; step 5.A's line rewritten; ADR 0029 written, ADR 0027 and ADR 0023 marked superseded in that part (the user).

Raised as: step 5.D, a rule clash with ADR 0027. Item (1) of the ruling The third repair of paper reads "one strategist agent per strand running the four layers, then a merge run". ADR 0027 ("A paper run is done by eight agent roles ...", its part in force) decides: "The strategist and the visualization agent are each started once as a fresh agent. A second message through the runner's message tool resumes each in these cases and no other." `skills/paper/SKILL.md:815` says the same. (a) A new ADR 0032, status proposed, supersedes 0027 in that part: in full, outline-only and lit-review mode the literature strategist is started once per strand the request names, the starts together, each running the four layers with the stopping conditions on its strand, and then once more as the merge agent, which removes duplicates, chains across strands and searches where strands meet; the four resumption cases resume the merge agent; revision mode keeps one start. Pros: each strand gets its own search and its own stop, the design of academic-paper, which `paper` was rebuilt from (its four theme strategists and its merge run); the resumptions keep one agent that holds the whole review. Cons: more agents and tokens per run. (b) Step 5.D follows ADR 0027: one strategist start that runs the four layers per strand in turn, with the stopping conditions per strand, and the merge as its last pass. Pros: no ADR change, fewer agents. Cons: one context holds every strand's searches, so the later strands get what is left of it, the condition under which the rc.5 run stopped with one clinical lesion-set source. Recommendation (a), since it is item (1) as ruled and the shape that collected the 147 sources; your ruling on (a) also approves ADR 0032 as worded here and the status line of 0027. The lazy option is (b).

The fixes: (1) a theme run hands on only leads (a source of a neighbouring area, a work it could not confirm, a search it could not run or finish), and a source it left out to hold its target is not handed on, as in academic-paper; (2) each theme run and merge job writes its annotations in `annotations-<name>.md`, the session joins them and the `.bib` files with `cat` into `annotations.md` and `refs.bib`, the merge run removes the duplicates and adds its own sources there by its own edits and writes the report's sections before and after the annotations as `review-part-1.md` and `review-part-2.md`, and the session joins the three with `cat` into `review.md`; (3) the jobs of the first citation round start with the leads jobs and the job `intersections`; (4) the synthesis start writes its section in at most one Edit per part of the research question. Carried in `SKILL.md` (Steps 8, lit-review items 1 and 3, the table of roles, "The prompts of a literature search"), the strategist page, the glossary, ADRs 0037 and 0041, both figure scripts, 9 figures, and the README and coverage descriptions.
- Fixes at landing after the stopped rc.15 run (2): the rc.15 run (`gate5i/lit-review-rc15`, stopped after 69 min of turn 2 on the user's go) showed `SKILL.md:1026`, "A source that passes every gate is included, and never held back", and the send-back rule at `:166` contradicting the theme run's target range and `:1063-1065`; its session sent theme run 2 back to assess the 239 sources it left out to hold its target. Now a theme run holds its target range, a source it leaves out above the top of its range is named in `review-theme-<k>.md` with that reason and is no work left undone, and a merge job and the single strategist have no range to hold (`SKILL.md:166`, `:1026`, ADR 0041 item 6). The strategist's read-only commands add `head`, `tail`, `cat`, `ls`, `sed -n` over several ranges, `cd` into its own folder and `echo` or `printf` of a label, and exclude a shell loop, a function and a variable holding a command (the strategist page's boundary, ADR 0041 item 1); in the rc.15 run theme run 3 was started fresh for a `sed -n` over two ranges and a `printf`, and theme run 2 sent back for `cd`, `echo`, `head`, `tail`, `cat` and `printf`.
